w14:paraId="46811EB1" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00A3749D">
w14:paraId="62834517" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00A3749D">HANDLING AND GENERAL PROCESSING NOTES FOR TIME AND TEMPERATURE SENSITIVE MATERIALS:
w14:paraId="60FE668F" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00A3749D">
w14:paraId="1AB168AC" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Refrigerated material is extremely fragile and shall be handled in a manner to avoid damage. When returning rolls of material into the freezer, prior to sealing, place at least 2 packets of desiccant in the box.
w14:paraId="23095777" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">For temperature sensitive materials: while in cold storage, and until the material is properly thawed as required in (f) below, the material shall be contained, fully sealed, either in the manufacturer’s storage bag or in clean/new polyethylene film.
w14:paraId="386D46EC" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Miscellaneous items, such as small tools, shall not be placed on top of kits of material or consumable production material.
w14:paraId="5346ABA4" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Rolls of prepreg and film adhesive shall be supported at all times by their cores, such that the material cannot be damaged from its own weight. 
w14:paraId="59339F99" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Containers of temperature sensitive materials such as prepregs and film adhesives shall only be opened (unsealed) in a controlled contamination area (CCA).
w14:paraId="2C7E9570" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">After being removed from cold storage, temperature sensitive materials shall be allowed to thaw and acclimate to the lay-up room ambient temperature while remaining in their sealed container. Materials must be allowed to thaw for sufficient time prior to removal from the packaging. In all cases, the material must have thawed to room temperature until there are no traces of condensation on the packaging for a minimum of 30 minutes. No form of applied or direct heat shall be used to accelerate the warming process.
w14:paraId="0F37AFE6" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">All materials removed from cold storage shall be completely resealed in either the original manufacturer’s packaging or a clean polyethylene vapor barrier material prior to returning to cold storage. Excess air shall be expelled from the container prior to sealing.
w14:paraId="6663C599" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Clean gloves must be worn at all times when handling time and temperature sensitive materials. Unless otherwise specified gloves must be made of powder free latex, or powder free nitrile rubber. The gloves must be non-contaminating to the composite fabrication process.
w14:paraId="1CA05D85" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Prior to use, cutting instruments shall be visually examined to verify that they are free of contamination from prepregs or film adhesives not used per this Router. Contaminated cutters shall be solvent wipe cleaned with acetone using a clean approved wiping cloth, or replaced prior to use. Cutters shall be replaced if there are signs the cutter is dull.
w14:paraId="4F30701D" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">If traces of moisture are found while unrolling the material for cutting or while examining a kit of material, the entire contents of the container will be considered suspect and be rejected on MRB CASE (this includes all plies cut from the time the container was last opened prior to the discovery of the moisture).
w14:paraId="1049D63D" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">The salvage edge shall be completely removed from all plies of pre-impregnated fabrics.
w14:paraId="7BACA37F" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Prepreg materials and film adhesives are marked, by the manufacturer, along the edge of the roll to point out any manufacturing defects. Any marked material shall be identified prior to the ply cutting activity and shall not be included in cut plies.
w14:paraId="290927F7" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Ply edges shall be clean after cutting. Slightly frayed or distorted edges shall be acceptable, provided that they will be outside the finished part edge after collation. Visual distortion of prepreg or film adhesive internal to the ply is unacceptable.
w14:paraId="5F4EE6B3" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Plies shall be visually examined for defects as they are cut. For pre-impregnated material, common defects include: fiber misalignment, cut or broken fibers, resin-rich areas, dry or boardy areas, smashes, fabric splices, and curled or folded salvage edges that overlap non-salvage areas. For film adhesives and pre-impregnated materials, common defects include: cured resin or hard spots, wrinkles, un-wetted areas and splices.
w14:paraId="70F5552A" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">For all plies, foreign matter is unacceptable. Foreign matter that is non-damaging to the base material may be removed. Sharp cutting instruments that may cause damage to the plies may not be used to remove foreign material. Flash breaker tape, nylon, uncoated aluminum, polyurethane, and polyethylene are examples of acceptable materials that may be used to remove foreign material from plies.
w14:paraId="0ED11AEE" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Operators shall wear Tyvek (or equivalent) jackets to prevent contact of street clothing with these prepreg materials.
w14:paraId="0702DAE1" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Designate an appropriate area to stack, store, and sort plies during cutting. Clean the cutting surface, equipment and sorting area to remove all debris prior to unrolling material.
w14:paraId="1393A8FB" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">One operator must watch while cutting plies. Verify that the knife does not cut into the salvage edge of the roll and that the material does not fold or warp causing a mis-cut. If any mis-cut plies are observed during cutting, the affected plies shall be discarded and recut.
w14:paraId="22C6645A" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00F63C60" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Part numbers for prepregs encompass the material type, class, grade/style in the naming nomenclature. This information will not be separately labeled on the Kit Cards.
w14:paraId="300488E5" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00A3749D" w:rsidRPr="00A56B1E" w:rsidRDefault="00A3749D" w:rsidP="00A3749D">
w14:paraId="39047582" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00CA41E1" w:rsidRPr="00CA41E1" w:rsidRDefault="00CA41E1" w:rsidP="00CA41E1">OP 100SUBCOMPONENT IDENTIFICATION

w14:paraId="2EAF2F8E" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="0054174C" w:rsidRPr="0054174C" w:rsidRDefault="0054174C" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Calculate individual material “Cure By” date/time based on remaining out time for material, unless shelf life (expiration date) is before out time runs out.
w14:paraId="11C031E1" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="0054174C" w:rsidRPr="0054174C" w:rsidRDefault="0054174C" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Calculate cure by date and time based on least remaining out time of material used, or based on shelf life if shelf life is less than least remaining out time. For this step use the material with the smallest number of out time hours based on when it was removed from the freezer. Divide the number of hours left by 24 and let the whole numbers be the number of days left and the decimal be the tenths of a day, so multiply that by 24 to get the number of hours remaining as a fraction of the last day.
w14:paraId="15464FAB" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="0054174C" w:rsidRPr="0054174C" w:rsidRDefault="0054174C" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">EXAMPLE of “Cure By” date/time based on remaining out time for material.
w14:paraId="456B1F2C" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="0054174C" w:rsidRPr="0054174C" w:rsidRDefault="0054174C" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Smallest out time of material used = 124 hours. Date and time removed from the freezer was Jan 31, 2024 at 7 am. Divide 124 by 24 which gives 5.1666, this says we have 5.166 days of out time left on the material. Take the .1666 and multiply by 24 which give us 4. So we have 5 days and 4 hours from the time the material was removed from the freezer to start the cure (based on out time). Add the 5 days and 4 hours to the date and time the material was removed from the freezer. 
w14:paraId="3997C2D6" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="0054174C" w:rsidRPr="0054174C" w:rsidRDefault="0054174C" w:rsidP="00CD42AD">Time out of freezer = Jan 31, 2024 at 7 am. Add 5 days and 4 hours = Feb 5 at 11 am. Now compare that to the shelf life expiration date for the material that expires the soonest, use the earliest date/time between shelf life and out time for the latest “Cure By” date and time to record in the router.

w14:paraId="545B4811" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00E33C11" w:rsidRPr="00D20CFD" w:rsidRDefault="00E33C11" w:rsidP="00E33C11">

w14:paraId="63C70B0A" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00E33C11" w:rsidRDefault="00E33C11" w:rsidP="00E33C11">

w14:paraId="1527AFA0" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00E33C11" w:rsidRPr="00D20CFD" w:rsidRDefault="00E33C11" w:rsidP="00E15E33">Wipe each coat in a crosshatch pattern and allow to air dry a minimum of (10) minutes between each coat. w14:paraId="2E894626" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00E33C11" w:rsidRPr="00D20CFD" w:rsidRDefault="00E33C11" w:rsidP="00E15E33">Record each mold release application including start and stop time/Date and oven temperature in the router
w14:paraId="6354DC0C" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00E33C11" w:rsidRPr="00D20CFD" w:rsidRDefault="00E33C11" w:rsidP="00E33C11">
w14:paraId="498A93B2" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00E33C11" w:rsidRDefault="00E33C11" w:rsidP="00E15E33">In the case of new or stripped molds, the Frekote 44 mold release agent (PN 00805017000F) must be baked prior to use in lamination. Mold temperature should be 70 - 90°F just prior to release application above. Apply 3 coats of Frekote 44 mold release agent (PN 00805017000F) in a cross - coat pattern, allowing each coat to flash off 15 minutes minimum prior to application of subsequent coats. After the release agent has been applied, bake the release layer for 60 minutes minimum at 140°F ± 10°F. Apply a fourth coat of release agent. Allow this coat to air dry at room temperature (minimum of 15 minutes). Buff mold prior to beginning layup.

w14:paraId="0633BD32" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00293BC8" w:rsidRPr="00E647BA" w:rsidRDefault="00293BC8" w:rsidP="00E15E33">Set the 3/8” ID Bushing in the Drill Fixture with the bushing tab facing up. 
w14:paraId="09EDDD44" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00293BC8" w:rsidRPr="00FB7F58" w:rsidRDefault="00293BC8" w:rsidP="00865C19">Locate the Drill Fixture on the front skin by placing the 3/8” Tee Pin through the 3/8” bushing and into the drill start in the front skin. See 
w14:paraId="054E32BA" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00293BC8" w:rsidRPr="00E647BA" w:rsidRDefault="00293BC8" w:rsidP="00E15E33">With the “Tee Pin” inserted in the drill start, hang a clamp on the right side of Drill Fixture and place the “Clamp Backup” against the back skin and tighten the clamp lightly. 
w14:paraId="23FCC5D1" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00293BC8" w:rsidRPr="00FC62A7" w:rsidRDefault="00293BC8" w:rsidP="00E15E33">Repeat the procedure on the left side of the Drill Fixture with the second clamp, apply just enough clamping force to hold the drill fixture in place. Ensure the “Tee Pin” is centered on the drill start and note the distance from the line on the “Tee Pin” to the top of the bushing, then lift it out of the drill start and reinsert it into the drill start. Note the position of the line again, if it’s not the same as previously observed realign the Drill Fixture.
w14:paraId="333D97E7" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00293BC8" w:rsidRPr="00FC62A7" w:rsidRDefault="00293BC8" w:rsidP="00E15E33">Use the 3/8” Twist Bit with a drill motor and drill through the core block letting the drill do the work and the bushing guide the drill bit as it sinks into the part until you meet the rear skin. w14:paraId="78C7D932" w14:textId="77777777" w:rsidR="00F40A0D" w:rsidRDefault="00F40A0D" w:rsidP="00E15E33">A pin-hole distribution of greater than 6 pin-holes per 16 square inch area on the reflector face and 10 per 16.0 sq. inch on other areas shall be grounds for rejection. Inclusions less than 0.1 inch across any dimension shall not be considered as defects, unless there are greater than two, 25.0 sq. inch areas on the face with greater than 6 inclusions less than 0.1 inch in size or greater than four, 25.0 sq. inch areas with greater than 6 inclusions less than 0.1 inch in size in all other areas on the reflector. Any symmetrical (non-string shaped) inclusion greater than 0.1 inches across any dimension on the reflector face shall be grounds for rejection. Any symmetrical inclusion greater than 0.13 inches across any dimension on the back and edge of the reflector of the reflector shall be grounds for rejection. Any string shaped inclusion on the reflector face greater than 0.5 inch in length and 0.03 inch in width shall be grounds for rejection. Any string shaped inclusion on the back and edge of the reflector greater than 0.75 inch in length and 0.03 inch in width shall be grounds for rejection. No blisters shall be present.
